=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given three length-N sequences of positive integers: A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N), B=(B_1,B_2,\ldots,B_N), and C=(C_1,C_2,\ldots,C_N).  
Find the number of pairs of positive integers (x, y) that satisfy the following condition:  

- A_i \times x + B_i \times y < C_i for all 1 \leq i \leq N.  

It can be proved that the number of such pairs of positive integers satisfying the condition is finite.  
You are given T test cases, each of which should be solved.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format. Here, \mathrm{case}_i refers to the i-th test case.
T  
\mathrm{case}_1  
\mathrm{case}_2  
\vdots  
\mathrm{case}_T  

Each test case is given in the following format:
N  
A_1 B_1 C_1  
A_2 B_2 C_2  
\vdots  
A_N B_N C_N

Output

Print T lines. The i-th line (1 \leq i \leq T) should contain the answer for \mathrm{case}_i.

Constraints


- 1 \leq T \leq 2 \times 10^5 
- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5 
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i, C_i \leq 10^9 
- The sum of N over all test cases is at most 2 \times 10^5.  
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2
2
1 1 4
1 2 5
1
1 1 2

Sample Output 1

2
0

In the first test case, there are two valid pairs of integers: (x, y) = (1, 1), (2,1). Thus, the first line should contain 2.  
In the second test case, there are no valid pairs of integers. Thus, the second line should contain 0.

Sample Output 2

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

AtCoder floor_sum(n, m, a, b) computes sum_{0<=i<n} floor((a*i + b)/m) for nonnegative a,b? It can handle a,b up to large but assumes 0<=a,b? The standard implementation reduces a,b modulo m and handles negative? Actually ACL floor_sum handles 0 <= a,b < m? It requires nonnegative? Let's recall:

def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans=0
    while True:
        if a>=m:
            ans += (n-1)*n*(a//m)//2
            a %= m
        if b>=m:
            ans += n*(b//m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a*n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max//m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans

This requires a,b >=0? It reduces a,b modulo m, but if a,b negative, Python % positive but a//m negative not handled? We can make a positive by using identity for negative a.

Detailed hull build:

def build_hull(lines):
    # lines sorted by (A/B asc, D/B asc)
    hull = []
    for A,B,D in lines:
        # skip same slope? We can handle in loop:
        if hull:
            lastA,lastB,lastD = hull[-1]
            if lastA * B == A * lastB:  # same slope
                # Since sorted by D/B asc, current has >= D/B, so skip
                continue
        # Now add line
        while len(hull) >= 2:
            A1,B1,D1 = hull[-2]
            A2,B2,D2 = hull[-1]
            # intersection x12 = (B1*D2 - B2*D1)/(A2*B1 - A1*B2)
            num12 = B1*D2 - B2*D1
            den12 = A2*B1 - A1*B2
            # intersection x23 = (B2*D_new - B_new*D2)/(A_new*B2 - A2*B_new)
            num23 = B2*D - B*D2
            den23 = A*B2 - A2*B
            # den positive? Since sorted A/B asc, A2/B2 < A/B => A2*B < A*B2 => den23>0.
            if num12 * den23 >= num23 * den12:
                hull.pop()
            else:
                break
        hull.append((A,B,D))
    return hull

But wait the skip same slope before adding: If current same slope as last, skip. But what if current same slope as some earlier but not last? Since sorted by slope, all same slope contiguous; if we skip, no later same slope added. Good.

However, if current same slope as last but lower D/B? Sorted by D/B ascending, so current cannot be lower. But our cmp tie sort by D/B ascending. Need ensure if same A/B, sorting by D/B ascending. If not, skip would be wrong. We'll implement cmp tie accordingly.

But there is a subtlety: For same slope, lower intercept line is D/B smaller. But if same slope and D/B smaller, it also has D/A smaller. The line with larger intercept might have smaller D//A? No, as argued no. But let's prove: A/B = k. D/B = c. D/A = c/k. Larger c -> larger D/A. So min X0 unaffected. Good.

Now, if we skip same slope after adding first, but what if first same slope has D/B not minimum because sort tie by D/B ascending? We'll ensure.

Now, cmp_to_key sorting by A/B ascending and D/B ascending. Let's define:

from functools import cmp_to_key
def cmp_line(p,q):
    A1,B1,D1 = p; A2,B2,D2 = q
    lhs = A1 * B2
    rhs = A2 * B1
    if lhs < rhs: return -1
    if lhs > rhs: return 1
    # same slope, compare D/B ascending
    lhs = D1 * B2
    rhs = D2 * B1
    if lhs < rhs: return -1
    if lhs > rhs: return 1
    return 0

lines.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp_line))

Potential issue: D up to 1e9-1, B up to 1e9, product 1e18 fits Python.

But could be dominated by another line with even lower values? If dominated, then min floor maybe from dominator. X0 from dominated line might still be smaller than dominator's zero? But X0 is min over all D_i//A_i; for line j, X0 <= D_j//A_j, so L_j(X0) >=0. Could be 0. So okay. If dominated line has smaller X0, then X0 is determined by it, and at x=X0 all lines nonnegative. The lower envelope line at x=X0 may be another line with value maybe 0? Since L_j <= L_i and L_i(X0)>=0, L_j could be negative? But X0 <= D_j//A_j ensures L_j(X0)>=0. So no negative. Good.

But wait X0 = min_i floor(D_i/A_i). For any line j, X0 <= floor(D_j/A_j), so D_j - A_j*X0 >=0. Thus L_j(X0) >=0. So all lines nonnegative at X0 and earlier. Good.

Now, let's scrutinize floor_sum implementation with large a,b. AtCoder floor_sum assumes 0 <= a,b < m? Actually the version with reductions:
def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans
This works for n>=0, m>=1, a,b>=0. Need ensure when a=0? If a=0, then y_max=b<m after reductions, break. If m,a swap with a=0? The loop breaks before swap because y_max < m. If a=0 and b>=m, reduce b, then y_max=b<m. Good.

Let's test floor_sum with n=2,m=2,a=1,b=2: a<m, b>=m => ans +=2*(1)=2, b=0; y_max=1*2+0=2 >=m; n=1, b=0, m,a = a,m => m=1,a=2. Loop: a>=m => ans += (0*1)*(2)//2=0, a=0; b>=m?0; y_max=0<1 break. ans=2. Good.

def cmp_line(p, q):
    A1,B1,D1 = p; A2,B2,D2 = q
    v = A1*B2 - A2*B1
    if v: return -1 if v<0 else 1
    v = D1*B2 - D2*B1
    if v: return -1 if v<0 else 1
    return 0
This sorts by A/B ascending, then D/B ascending. Good.

def cmp_line(p, q):
    v = p[0]*q[1] - q[0]*p[1]
    if v < 0: return -1
    if v > 0: return 1
    v = p[2]*q[1] - q[2]*p[1]
    if v < 0: return -1
    if v > 0: return 1
    return 0
This avoids unpacking. Good.

Let's write the code in mind:

import sys
from functools import cmp_to_key

def cmp_line(p, q):
    v = p[0]*q[1] - q[0]*p[1]
    if v < 0: return -1
    if v > 0: return 1
    v = p[2]*q[1] - q[2]*p[1]
    if v < 0: return -1
    if v > 0: return 1
    return 0

def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    it = iter(data)
    T = next(it)
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N = next(it)
        lines = []
        X0 = 10**30
        for _ in range(N):
            A = next(it); B = next(it); C = next(it)
            D = C - 1
            lines.append((A,B,D))
            lim = D // A
            if lim < X0:
                X0 = lim
        if X0 <= 0:
            out.append("0")
            continue
        lines.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp_line))
        hull = []
        for A,B,D in lines:
            if hull and hull[-1][0] * B == A * hull[-1][1]:
                # same slope; sorted by D/B ascending, so current is not better
                continue
            while len(hull) >= 2:
                A1,B1,D1 = hull[-2]
                A2,B2,D2 = hull[-1]
                num12 = B1*D2 - B2*D1
                den12 = A2*B1 - A1*B2
                num23 = B2*D - B*D2
                den23 = A*B2 - A2*B
                if num12 * den23 >= num23 * den12:
                    hull.pop()
                else:
                    break
            hull.append((A,B,D))
        ans = 0
        start = 1
        # local fs
        fs = floor_sum
        for idx, (A,B,D) in enumerate(hull):
            if idx + 1 < len(hull):
                A2,B2,D2 = hull[idx+1]
                num = B*D2 - B2*D
                den = A2*B - A*B2
                end = num // den
                if end > X0:
                    end = X0
            else:
                end = X0
            if start <= end:
                n = end - start + 1
                b = D - A * end
                ans += fs(n, B, A, b)
            start = max(start, end + 1)
            if start > X0:
                break
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with using `lines.sort(key=lambda p: p[0] * SCALE // p[1])` and then grouping by cross multiplication; if same slope group not contiguous due to key equal but sort stable? Equal keys are contiguous because sort groups by key. Yes.

Now, let's update final code:

import sys

SCALE = 10**18

def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans

def solve():
    it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    try:
        T = next(it)
    except StopIteration:
        return
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N = next(it)
        lines = []
        X0 = 10**30
        for _ in range(N):
            A = next(it); B = next(it); C = next(it)
            D = C - 1
            lines.append((A,B,D))
            lim = D // A
            if lim < X0:
                X0 = lim
        if X0 <= 0:
            out.append("0")
            continue
        if N == 1:
            A,B,D = lines[0]
            ans = floor_sum(X0, B, A, D - A * X0)
            out.append(str(ans))
            continue
        lines.sort(key=lambda p: p[0] * SCALE // p[1])
        # group same slope, keep smallest D/B
        unique = []
        i = 0
        L = len(lines)
        while i < L:
            A,B,D = lines[i]
            i += 1
            while i < L and lines[i][0] * B == A * lines[i][1]:
                A2,B2,D2 = lines[i]
                if D2 * B < D * B2:
                    A,B,D = A2,B2,D2
                i += 1
            unique.append((A,B,D))
        lines = unique
        hull = []
        for A,B,D in lines:
            while len(hull) >= 2:
                A1,B1,D1 = hull[-2]
                A2,B2,D2 = hull[-1]
                num12 = B1*D2 - B2*D1
                den12 = A2*B1 - A1*B2
                num23 = B2*D - B*D2
                den23 = A*B2 - A2*B
                if num12 * den23 >= num23 * den12:
                    hull.pop()
                else:
                    break
            hull.append((A,B,D))
        ans = 0
        start = 1
        fs = floor_sum
        H = len(hull)
        for idx, (A,B,D) in enumerate(hull):
            if idx + 1 < H:
                A2,B2,D2 = hull[idx+1]
                num = B*D2 - B2*D
                den = A2*B - A*B2
                end = num // den
                if end > X0:
                    end = X0
            else:
                end = X0
            if start <= end:
                n = end - start + 1
                b = D - A * end
                ans += fs(n, B, A, b)
            start = max(start, end + 1)
            if start > X0:
                break
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()

But wait, what if A/B values differ by exactly 1e-18 but scaled diff exactly 1, floor keys differ. Good. Yes. So >=1/(BD) >=1e-18. Good.

B=1e9,D=1e9, fractions a/1e9 and c/1e9 with |a-c|=1 => difference=1e-9, not 1e-18. To get difference=1e-18, need denominator product=1e18 and numerator=1, e.g. 1/(1e9) and 0/1? But A positive, no 0. Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that the code's `lines.sort(key=lambda p: p[0] * SCALE // p[1])` for N large and SCALE global; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with using `
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

SCALE = 10**18


def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    """sum_{i=0}^{n-1} floor((a*i + b) / m), for n >= 0, m > 0, a,b >= 0"""
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans


def solve():
    it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    try:
        T = next(it)
    except StopIteration:
        return

    out = []

    for _case in range(T):
        N = next(it)
        lines = []
        X0 = 10**30

        for _i in range(N):
            A = next(it)
            B = next(it)
            C = next(it)
            D = C - 1
            lines.append((A, B, D))
            lim = D // A
            if lim < X0:
                X0 = lim

        if X0 <= 0:
            out.append("0")
            continue

        if N == 1:
            A, B, D = lines[0]
            ans = floor_sum(X0, B, A, D - A * X0)
            out.append(str(ans))
            continue

        # Exact sort by A/B using a large enough scale.
        # Since A,B <= 1e9, distinct fractions differ by at least 1e-18.
        lines.sort(key=lambda p: p[0] * SCALE // p[1])

        # Group equal slopes, keeping the smallest D/B.
        unique = []
        i = 0
        L = len(lines)
        while i < L:
            A, B, D = lines[i]
            i += 1
            while i < L and lines[i][0] * B == A * lines[i][1]:
                A2, B2, D2 = lines[i]
                if D2 * B < D * B2:
                    A, B, D = A2, B2, D2
                i += 1
            unique.append((A, B, D))

        lines = unique

        # Lower envelope of lines L(x) = (D - A*x) / B.
        # Slopes -A/B are decreasing because A/B is increasing.
        hull = []
        for A, B, D in lines:
            while len(hull) >= 2:
                A1, B1, D1 = hull[-2]
                A2, B2, D2 = hull[-1]

                num12 = B1 * D2 - B2 * D1
                den12 = A2 * B1 - A1 * B2

                num23 = B2 * D - B * D2
                den23 = A * B2 - A2 * B

                if num12 * den23 >= num23 * den12:
                    hull.pop()
                else:
                    break
            hull.append((A, B, D))

        ans = 0
        start = 1
        H = len(hull)

        for idx, (A, B, D) in enumerate(hull):
            if idx + 1 < H:
                A2, B2, D2 = hull[idx + 1]
                num = B * D2 - B2 * D
                den = A2 * B - A * B2
                end = num // den
                if end > X0:
                    end = X0
            else:
                end = X0

            if start <= end:
                n = end - start + 1
                b = D - A * end
                ans += floor_sum(n, B, A, b)

            start = max(start, end + 1)
            if start > X0:
                break

        out.append(str(ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
